先週（11/2〜11/9）発表の主な経済指標
⽶国雇⽤は安定も、AI曝露度の⾼い職業で若年雇⽤が弱含
み。雇⽤不安が⺠主党躍進を後押しした可能性。AI関連株が
調整するなか、韓国中銀は投資減速のアジアへの波及を警戒
所定内給与は物価上昇率を上回る伸びが持続。冬季ボーナス
も押し上げ。電気・ガス料⾦補助再開は消費の追い⾵に。⼀⽅、
消費回復が進めば基調的な物価上昇圧⼒を強める可能性
注目点② 賃上げの波及と物価対策が日本の消費を下支え注目点① 米AI投資拡大の裏で広がる「不安」と「リスク」
⾮農業部⾨雇⽤者数は市場予想を上回り、失業
率も低下。ISM景況指数は製造業・⾮製造業とも
に拡⼤圏を維持。雇⽤・企業活動の底堅さの⼀⽅
で、消費者マインドは持ち直すも依然として低⽔準

（出所）米労働省より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
失業率
米国：雇用統計は良好な結果。一方で、賃金上昇もインフレ見合いで見劣り
 10月の非農業部門雇用者数は前月差＋16.5万人と市場予想（同＋6.5万人）を大きく上回る結果
―過去2カ月も合わせて8.4万人の上方修正。業種別では専門ビジネスサービスやヘルスケアが全体をけん引
― 時間当たり賃金も前月比＋0.4％（前年比＋3.1％）と着実に増加。ただしインフレ率に割り負けている状況が継続
 失業率は4.1％と前月（4.2％）から小幅低下も、失業期間は長期化
― 失業期間の中央値は11.9週（9月：同11.5週）と長期化。27週以上の長期失業者も増加基調維持。企業の採用率は低下

傾向が続いており、労働者が失業状態から脱しにくい傾向が継続
3
（出所）米労働省より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
非農業部門雇用者数
失業率は低位安定
雇用者数は市場予想を大きく上回る
過去2カ月もそれぞれ上方修正

（2022年11月=100）
（年）
米国：AI普及の影響は若年層に偏在。雇用機会への不安が拡大
 AI曝露度別の雇用指数をみると、22～34歳では曝露度の高い職業の雇用が相対的に弱含み
― AIの影響がキャリア形成段階の若年層に偏っている可能性
 若年層では、AIによる雇用代替に加え、物価高など生活面の不安も根強い状況
― AIを否定的に捉える割合も若い世代ほど高く、AI普及への警戒感がうかがえる状況
4
（注） 上図の若年層とは18～29歳。経済状況は回答者の主観的な評価

（出所） Harvard Youth Poll、ABC News、Washington Post、Ipsosより、みずほ総合研
究所調査部作成
AI曝露度別雇用指数
年齢別AIの認識：「AIは社会に悪い」と回答した割合
（注） 1．AI曝露度とは各職業の業務が生成AIの影響を受ける度合いを示す。職業を曝露度
に応じて5分位に分類し、低・中・高はそれぞれ第1・第3・第5分位を指す
2．年齢層別の雇用指数は、米国雇用統計の家計調査における年齢別雇用者数をウェ
イトに加重平均して算出。年齢区分が一致しない部分は、各階級内の雇用者数が均
等に分布すると仮定して按分
（出所）スタンフォード大学デジタル経済研究所、ADP Researchより、みずほ総合研究所調査
部作成
中堅・シニア層（35歳以上）
若年層（22～34歳）
若年層が感じる経済的脅威
若年層ほどAIへの反感が強い
若年層に偏る雇用への影響

（年/月）
米国：若年層の経済的不安が中間選挙の投票行動にも影響か
 2026年中間選挙では「物価高」や「雇用」への不安が若年層の主要な関心事項に
― こうした経済的不安が共和党政権への不満につながった可能性
― 年齢別の投票動向では、若年層を中心に民主党支持が優勢。生活費負担や将来の雇用機会への不安が投票行動に
影響した模様
― なお、議会でも世代交代が進展。若い世代の政策選好が今後の政策形成に与える影響にも注目

### Page 7

（参考）民主党が掲げる主な政策
 勝利した民主党は、若年層の不満とも重なる生活費負担の軽減（アフォーダビリティ）を共通の政策課題に位置付け
 具体的な政策手段を巡っては、中道派と左派・進歩派の間で違い
― 中道派は競争促進や規制緩和、財政規律を重視する一方、左派・進歩派は大企業への規制・課税強化や政府支出の
拡大を主張。今後の党内調整が焦点に
6

（年）

アジア：AI投資減速の影響が域内の貿易ネットワークを通じて波及するリスク
 韓国中銀はAI関連投資の拡大に伴う過剰投資やレバレッジの高まり、循環金融などの金融リスクを警戒
― AI産業の期待収益の過大評価や最終的なリスク所在把握が困難になり、リスクが過小評価される可能性があると指摘
― また、米国企業のAI投資が減速する悲観シナリオでは、韓国の半導体輸出が大幅に減少し、GDP成長率を0.4％Pt押し
下げると試算
― 韓国のAI関連財貿易は中国・台湾との取引が4割超。アジア域内で形成された貿易ネットワークを通じて、AI投資減速の
影響が広範に波及する可能性

日本：物価対策の再開が家計負担を軽減。消費の回復を後押し
9
 政府は冬場の電気・ガス料金補助の再開を決定。標準的な家庭で計7,000円程度の負担軽減を見込む
― 一方、ガソリン価格の補助は段階的に縮小（基準額を毎月5円ずつ引き上げ）。電気・ガス料金補助金の効果を一部減殺
 補助金を巡る政策変更は、コアCPIの前年比を最大0.4％程度押し下げ
― 賃金上昇に加え、政策による物価負担軽減が家計の実質購買力を下支え
― 物価対策がCPIを押し下げる一方、消費回復を通じて基調的な物価上昇圧力が強まる可能性には留意が必要

（注） ガソリンと同額の補助を実施している軽油・重油・灯油、ガソリン補助額の4割相当

欧州：10月消費者物価はエネルギー主導で加速。企業は販売価格の引き上げを計画
10
 10月消費者物価（HICP）は前年比＋4.0％と、エネルギー主導で前月（同＋3.8％）から加速
― 一方、コアは同＋2.5％と前月から不変。エネルギー高の二次的な影響はいまだ顕在化せず
 コスト上昇圧力が高まるなか、企業は販売価格の引き上げを計画。高止まりするインフレにECBは警戒姿勢を維持

中国：政策金利をリバースレポ翌日物に変更。銀行貸出金利の新たな決定メカニズムを模索
 中国人民銀行は政策金利をリバースレポ翌日物に変更（11/2）。短期市場金利の誘導精度を高める狙い
― リバースレポ7日物から切り替え、翌日物のオペを毎営業日実施。最優遇貸出金利（LPR）も翌日物金利を参照へ
― インターバンク市場における債券担保付レポ翌日物金利（DR001）が誘導目標と明言。短期金利の安定化を図る考え
 銀行貸出金利の新たな決定メカニズムを模索。DR001を参照して貸出金利を決定する方法を試験的に導入

― 背景には、銀行貸出の指標金利であるLPRが「最優遇」から乖離し、LPRより低利での貸出比率が上昇している実態
― 今後、財務力のある企業は銀行と協議の上でDR001かLPRを選択し、小規模企業や個人には引き続きLPRを適用へ

― うち非製造業PMIが49.7と前月（50.2）から低下も、宿泊やスポーツ・娯楽が持ち直し、情報サービスは堅調だった

― 中東情勢に伴う価格上昇に一服感。原材料価格は56.8（同：60.8）、出荷価格は51.7（同：54.0）まで戻す

中国：PPIは生産財が急上昇も、川下への価格転嫁進まず。CPIは食品価格が下押し要因に

### Page 15

アジア：ASEAN5の製造業景況感は概ね堅調。原油高など仕入コスト上昇の影響は残存
 ASEAN5の10月の製造業景況感は、フィリピンを除き改善圏。受注・生産は好調も、コスト高が継続
― タイ、ベトナムは高水準で推移し、インドネシアは回復が鮮明。一方、フィリピンが基準となる50を下回る水準
― S&Pはリリース資料において、新規受注の増加が生産を支えたものの、仕入コスト上昇と原料調達・物流の制約を指摘
 ASEAN5では、生産者物価（PPI）が高止まり。原油高の影響が残存
― 原油高が続く中、石炭・石油に加えて化学やプラ・ゴム等の製品価格にも上昇圧力が継続
― 仕入コストの高止まりが、企業収益や生産活動の重荷となるリスクに留意が必要

 ASEAN5の一部の国で財政収支が悪化

― フィリピンは税収の伸び悩み、タイは景気下支えに向けた公共投資拡大や予算執行の加速などにより歳出が大きく増加

## Document 122: 261110_daiwa_gce

Source PDF: all report pdf/261110_daiwa_gce.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-10
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: We review GCE’s 3Q26 results, released on 10 November.

What’s the impact: 3Q26 beat Bloomberg consensus. GCE’s 3Q26 
earnings came in at TWD6.39bn (EPS: TWD12.4), in line with our estimate 
and 3% higher vs. the street’s. While revenue was 7% lower than our 
expectation due to flooding in Thailand and product transition, gross margin 
expanded by 4pp QoQ to 38.7% mainly due to easing raw-material cost 
inflation and its own price increases. However, net income was impacted by 
higher non-op loss of TWD238m (likely due to FX loss) though offset by a 
lower effective tax rate of 25% as Thailand plant turned more profitable.

Business outlook. Looking ahead, GCE now guides for revenue to be 
flattish QoQ in 4Q26. We suspect that AMZN’s ASIC AI server gradually 
shifting its model from air-cooled (2 chipsets per mainboard) to liquid-
cooled (4 chipsets) and the year-end inventory-taking are the key reasons. 
However, we expect the stagnant revenue momentum to be short-lived and 
growth momentum to resume with new projects (including Google’s TPU 
and MSFT’s Maia) contributing from 1H27.

In 2H27, we expect new capacity ramps at its 2nd plant in Thailand and 2nd 
plant in Suzhou (acquisition of Pegatron’s land; across the 1st plant in 
Suzhou) to be growth drivers, which could further increase its allocation in 
major ASIC AI server projects. As for concerns over the thicker-HDI board 
(a combination of sequential lamination HLC and HDI together) that we 
highlighted in in our previous note (2Q26 results review: profitability missed 
slightly), we believe GCE has already overcome the difficulties for 3-
lamination HDI and can now successfully produce AMZN’s requirement on 
its Trn4 AI server heavy HDI board. However, GCE might still need to 
achieve the requirement for Google’s TPU v9 which requires c.5-6 
lamination techniques. We believe the capacity expansion in 2027 would 
not be enough to catch up with AI demand growth in 2027. As such, we 
believe it will further drive price hikes in CCL raw-material, CCL, and PCB, 
and see gross margin (GM) expansion over our forecast horizon. We raise 
our GM forecasts to 40.5%/41.3% (prior: 38.0/40.2%) for 2027-28E.

What we recommend: We raise our 2027-28E EPS by 1-10% to reflect its 
3Q26 results and higher GM on current supply-demand dynamics. We 
reaffirm our Buy (1) call but trim our 12M TP to TWD1,600 with a lower 
PER target of 21x (prior: 26x; vs. past-3-year range of 7-28x), on our 4-
quarter-forward diluted EPS, to reflect weaker market sentiment and on-
going concerns over HDI. Key risk: worse-than-expected AI server demand.

How we differ: Our 2027-28E earnings are 4-5% higher than consensus, 
likely due to our higher profitability assumptions.

10 November 2026

3Q26 review: results largely in line with our estimates

➢ GM expands with spec upgrade and favourable pricing environment 
➢ New client and new products likely to be key growth drivers in 2027 
➢ Maintaining Buy (1) rating; trimming 12M TP to TWD1,600

Company profile 
Founded in 1981 and listed on the Taiwan Stock Exchange in 1998, Gold Circuit Electronics (GCE) was, 
in its early years, one of the world’s top-3 printed circuit board (PCB) vendors providing double-sided 
and multi-layer PCBs for notebooks. However, as the notebook market became saturated from 2011, 
GCE started reshuffling its revenue mix to more high-end products (servers, workstations and 
networking boards). The requirement of higher-layer PCBs from these products has not only proved 
GCE’s manufacturing capabilities but also created a high entry barrier. GCE’s revenue contribution from 
the server and networking segments has surged to over 80% in 2024 (from 37% in 2010), the highest 
among its PCB peers.

### Page 5

5

Gold Circuit Electronics (2368 TT): 10 November 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 10 Nov 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 2
GCE's Board consists of 9 directors, including 3 independent directors. The number of
independent directors is above Taiwan securities law's requirement of more than two and
exceeds one-fifth of the total number of directors on the board. The backgrounds of the
independent directors are diverse with expertise in the areas of business management, finance
& audit, and law, which should be positive for executive and board quality.
That said, the roles of CEO and chairman are held by one person, who is a second-generation
family member of GCE’s founder. The organisation structure appears to us to be a family
business, which could lead to conflicts of interest between key executives and outside
shareholders.
Capital management 2
GCE declared a DPS of TWD10.0 in 2025, another record high. GCE has declared cash
dividends for six consecutive years from 2020 to 2025, following a period of intermittent payouts
mainly due to fierce competition in the PCB industry (especially in notebook applications) and
high earnings volatility from its 3 plants in China caused by historically low utilisation rates. We
believe the company’s strategy to focus on high-end products (eg, servers and networking) has
started bearing fruit, which was also the main reason GCE was able to declare a record-high
cash dividend in 2025. Overall, we expect GCE to continue to benefit from ASP and gross-
margin expansion, driven by server and networking spec upgrades, which should be value-
accretive for investors in the long term.
Related party &
transaction 1 Sales to related parties were 0% of total 2025 sales and purchases from related parties were also 0% of total 2025 purchases. We see limited risk from related-party transactions.
S Supply chain management 1
GCE is a multi-layer PCB manufacturer with major raw materials, such as thin core, copper foil,
pre-preg, copper ball, dry film, solder mask and various chemical materials used in the
manufacturing process. GCE has acquired the ISO 9001 and IECQ QC 080000 certifications to
improve its supply chain management capabilities in product quality and hazardous material
usage. Like most hardware vendors, GCE is also committed to refusing procurement of conflict
minerals and complying with the Responsible Business Alliance (RBA) Code of Conduct.
According to its 2025 annual report, over 99% of its top 40 suppliers returned their RBA
commitment letters. As part of its RBA compliance efforts, GCE holds an annual supply chain
meeting to review the achievement rate of its previous targets and set new goals for suppliers. In
2025, GCE audited 154 suppliers, including 114 on-site audits and 40 document reviews,
achieving a 100% completion rate against its audit targets. The company also evaluates new
suppliers based on ISO 9001, ISO 14001 and IATF 16949 certification requirements and
conducts annual supplier assessments.
S Data security 2
Information security is one of the key topics under the company’s risk management guidelines.
GCE constructed an information security system, under the “information security committee”, to
minimise the risk of information loss, sabotage, and leakage caused by human errors, malicious
destructions or natural disasters. Below are several measures the company has taken to
improve data security: GCE will conduct regular awareness training to ensure employees'
security awareness and fulfilment of information security system requirements. Also, the
information security committee will develop an information security continuity plan and conduct
drills to ensure continuous operation of the company.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

### Page 3

Hon Hai Precision (2317.TW)
12 November 2026 Citi Research
3
CPO: New growth opportunities from vertical integration — Hon Hai sees
significant long-term opportunities in both ASIC and vertical integration including
optical interconnect technologies. In optical networking, management believes the
transition from electrical to optical interconnects is structurally inevitable as AI
models expand and memory-bandwidth requirements increase. Hon Hai’s CPO

optical switching already starts mass volume production for its major customer,
and its micron-level precision manufacturing expertise in optical alignment,
connectors, and electrical-to-optical conversion could create additional value
through component integration and higher system-level content. Its extensive
engineering resources, ODM/JDM development capabilities, and vertically
integrated manufacturing platform should strengthen its competitive positioning.

Management expressed strong confidence in optical growth opportunities but did
not provide specific 2027 revenue guidance.

Robust financials and earnings growth, maintain Buy — Thanks to strong
demand from major CSP clients and its continuous automation and vertical
integration development, Hon Hai’s capex increased 43% YTD. Nevertheless, its
EBITDA increased 50.3% YoY to NT$369.2bn. To improve capital efficiency, Hon

Hai plans to expand consignment arrangements, optimize inventory and
receivables management, and carefully evaluate investment payback periods.
Management reiterated its long-term operating margin floor of above 3%, with
further potential upside from scale economies, automation, product mix
improvements, and greater vertical integration.
Given Hon Hai’s solid result and better operational leverage, we raise our
2026/2027/2028 EPS by 4%/5%/5%. Considering its solid execution, stable

dividend yield and attractive valuation, we reiterate our Buy rating with TP NT$400
unchanged. Our TP implies 15x of our 2027 EPS projection.

### Page 9

Hon Hai Precision (2317.TW)
12 November 2026 Citi Research
9
Hon Hai Precision
Company description
Established in 1974, Hon Hai has become the largest EMS player in the world
in terms of sales. Through its vertical integration model or "eCMMS" (e-
enabled Components, Modules, Moves and Services), it provides
components, modules and system assembly products to many global OEMs
in the PC, communication and consumer sectors.
Investment strategy
We rate Hon Hai shares as Buy as we believe the company’s margins will

continue to improve, backed by a renewed focus on profitability, operational
efficiency and new businesses development. We think there’s more upside to
go from current level, as the underlying drivers are not just about product
cycle, but also about fundamental transformation and new business
opportunities.
Valuation
Our target price of NT$400 is based on 15x average 2026E-27E EPS, which is
at the higher end of the historical trading range. We believe this multiple is

justified by the company’s enhanced sales growth and profit margin outlook,
as well as new endeavors into AI server, EV, digital health and robotics. Hon
Hai has been able to generate positive earnings streams, and thus we believe
a P/E valuation methodology is appropriate.
Risks
Downside risks that could impede the stock from reaching our target price
include: 1) slower-than-expected iPhone demand; 2) intensified peer
competition; 3) weaker-than-expected execution on new business progress;

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds

## Document 169: 261112_ms_accton

### Page 1

M
Idea
3Q26 GM slightly lower: Off revenue of NT$120,744mn (+26% QoQ/+66% YoY),
GM contracted by 2.1ppt QoQ to 17.6% (vs. MSe and consensus both at 18.5%) on
higher component costs. With opex of NT$5,162mn (opex ratio 4.3% vs. MSe 4.3%/
consensus  4.0%), OP was NT$16,122mn (OPM 13.4%), 6%/5% lower than MSe of NT
$17,183mn (OPM 14.2%)/consensus  of NT$17,047mn (OPM 14.4%), respectively. With
a narrower non-op gain of NT$94mn, net profit came in at NT$13,074mn (EPS NT

$23.4), 2%/5% short of MSe/consensus of NT$13,328mn (EPS NT$23.9)/NT
$13,748mn (EPS NT$24.5).
Outlook still on optimistic side: We expect Accton to grow revenue sequentially
into 4Q26. Network switch customers will likely continue to pull in more for their AI
clusters amid 800G migration. Meanwhile, its AI accelerator customer should ramp
more of its new generation product and increase adoption of liquid cooling thermal
solutions. To mitigate pressure from elevated component costs, it has negotiated

with customers for them to consign some components, e.g., DRAM, from late 3Q26.
Although this might cap revenue growth upside for Accton, it should help on the
GM side. We model revenue of NT$130,083mn (+8% QoQ/+81% YoY), gross margin
of 20.0% (+2.4ppt QoQ), operating margin of 15.5% (+2.1ppt QoQ)  and EPS of NT
$28.2 (+21% QoQ/+88% YoY). Looking to 2027, we see revenue growth of NT
$526,019mn (+26% YoY) and GM of 20.0%, for EPS NT$112.9 EPS (+31% YoY).

Stay OW: We believe Accton should continue to benefit from networking and
compute demand growth amid AI deployment, reflecting its system integration
capability, customer relationships, and execution. For the longer term,  it will likely
tap into opportunities in rack level solutions, optical-based switches, etc. We lower
our PT to NT$2,250 (20x 2027 P/E) from NT$3,500 (30x 2027 P/E), mostly owing
to more conservative sentiment toward AI names.
What’s Changed

Accton Technology Corporation (2345.TW) From To
Price Target NT$3,500.00 NT$2,250.00
Accton Technology Corporation  | Asia Pacific
3Q26 GM Fell Short Slightly;
Better Margin Trend Ahead
Although 3Q26 earnings came up short on lower GM, we expect
Accton to deliver high single digit % revenue growth with GM
back to ~20% in 4Q26, reflecting some component consignment
arrangements.  Margin expansion in 4Q26 should be a positive
catalyst for the share price in the coming months.

### Page 6

Idea
6
Valuation Methodology and Risks
OW with PT of NT$2,250 (previously NT$3,500)
Price target (base case scenario value) –  NT$2,250 (-36%): Our new price target is based
on 20x our 2027 EPS estimate (30x previously). We lower our target multiple to factor in
recent market sentiment toward AI names. We see our target multiple supported by the
45% operating profit CAGR we project for 2025-28. This implies a PEG of 0.4 vs. its
average of 0.7 over 2019-25, when the stock was trading at a P/E of 22x for an operating

profit CAGR of 32%.
Bull case scenario value – NT$3,350 (-20%): We apply 30x to our base case EPS estimate
for 2027 (36x previously). This scenario reflects stronger demand for network switches in
both data center and enterprise applications as well as more orders for AI accelerator
modules.
Bear case scenario value – NT$1,800 (-39%): We apply 10x to our base case EPS estimate
for 2027 (16x previously). This scenario reflects lukewarm data center and enterprise

capex and continued dominance by merchant GPUs in AI servers.
Downside risks to our positive thesis
• Weaker data center capex
• Slower network switch spec migration
• InfiniBand taking more share in AI networks
• Softer custom AI accelerator growth
• Softer ODM direct business model adoptions

Risk Reward - Accton Technology Corporation (2345.TW)
NT$2,250.00
Base case, P/E, which we believe better captures the company's earnings growth momentum.
We assign a target P/E of 20x to our 2027 EPS estimate, which we view as justiﬁed by an
operating proﬁt CAGR of 45% for 2025-28, based on our estimates. This implies a PEG of
around 0.4, vs. its average of 0.7 over 2019-25, when the stock was trading at a P/E of 22x for
an operating proﬁt CAGR of 32%.

Consensus Price Target Distribution NT$2,250.00 NT$3,700.00

MS Rating
▪ We believe Accton, as one of the major
network switch suppliers in the ODM direct
market, will continue to gain from increasing
data trafﬁc (22% CAGR seen for 2023-30)
and subsequent spec upgrades for
processing throughput.
▪ We think that in addition to existing
customers, it will leverage the open-source

network operating system to broaden its
customer base.
▪ We see the network switch business
emerging as the major revenue driver in
2026, supported by increasing growth from
its existing customer and incremental
contribution from another hyperscaler.
▪ Our PT of NT$2,250 is based on a target
P/E multiple of 20x 2027e.

addition, Ethernet technology loses market

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views

below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

### Page 1

Update
Co-Tech Development Corp.  | Asia Pacific
Key Japanese Competitor
Announced Price Hikes
What's new? Our supply chain checks suggest that one of Co-Tech's key Japanese
competitors notified customers earlier this week of price increases for its
electrodeposited (ED) copper foil products. The company will raise prices by 30%
for ¼ oz (9µm) and ⅓ oz (12µm) ED copper foils and by 15% for other thicknesses,
effective for shipments from December 2026 onward. This aligns with our previous

supply chain check findings published in our Nov 4 report (link).
Our view: The increases extend beyond high-end HVLP products to copper foils
used in consumer electronics. We believe this reflects rising production costs and,
more importantly, tightening supply, with positive implications for Co-Tech. We
expect Co-Tech to follow with further price increases, although likely at smaller
magnitudes given the ~10% hike it implemented in October.
Morgan Stanley Taiwan Limited+

Rachel L Zhang
Equity Analyst

Key Takeaways
Earlier this week, a key Japanese competitor issued a price increase notice to its
customers for electrodeposited copper foils.
This aligns with our  supply chain check findings published in our Nov 4 report.
We believe the  price hikes have positive implications for Co-Tech and expect Co-
Tech to follow with price increases as well.

However, since Co-Tech already implemented a ~10% price hike in October, we
don't expect it to announce a price hike of the same magnitude.

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

4971.TT IET-KY 
 400G 磊晶、QD Laser 的先鋒

[法說會更新]   
   公司簡介 
英特磊科技股份有限公司(IET-KY)於 2011 年 4 月設立，為全球磷化銦(InP)、銻化鎵
(GaAs)、銻化鎵(GaSb)磊晶片代工領導廠，提供無線通訊、光纖通訊等產品應用。3Q26
營收組成為 GaAs 36.5%、InP 33.4%、GaSb 和勞務收入 26.5%與其它 3.5%。 
投資評等/目標價 重點摘要 
區間操作 424 
【前次投資建議】無 
【大盤指數/股價】43092 / 400

➢ 3Q26 出貨量回升，4Q26 認列設備營收，2026 年營收年增 17.4% 
由於 InP 基板供貨恢復穩定使 Epi-Wafer 出貨量成長，IET-KY 3Q26 營
收 3.2 億元(YoY+26.8%，QoQ+24.6%)。毛利率 38.7%(YoY+7ppt)提
升係因高毛利水準之磊晶片及硬體銷售所帶動。EPS 1.26 元。3Q26 營
收組成為 GaAs 36.5%、InP 33.4%、GaSb 和勞務收入 26.5%與其它
3.5%。各項產品線營運狀況如下：(1)GaAs：射頻與國防應用合計占其
比重約 20%，營收年增 72%。(2)InP：APD/PD 需求極為強勁，但因基
板供應受限，營收年減 2%。考量客製化修改特殊設備營收預計於 12 月
認列，且基板供貨穩定，研究員預期 12 月營收高於 10~11 月，預計
4Q26 營收季增 12.4%至 3.6 億元(YoY+12.7%)，預估 2026 年營收 12.7
億元(YoY+17.4%)，毛利率 40%，EPS 4.85 元。 
➢ 2027 年 400G EAM 與 Driver 開始量產，產能年增 10%至 20% 
展望 2027 年，GaAs 方面，國防訂單與商用訂單同步增加 30%。VCSEL
在新增機台後產能估約增加 40%，預估 GaAs 營收年增 20%至 25%。
第一位客戶已突破 InP 基板限制後，預計 2027 年開始量產 400G EAM
與 Driver。若 2027 年基板供應能維持現有水準，預 期 InP Epi-Wafer 出
貨量將有雙位數成長。在 GaSb 方面，公司著重於生物辨識等創新應用，
並已建置第二條 GaSb 生產線，預計於 2027 年正式量產。配合晶片法
案承諾，公司將於 2027 年購買 2 至 3 台機台，機台總數增至 18 台至
19 台，總產能約增加 10%至 20%，預估 2027 年營收 16.1 億元
(YoY+27.1%)，毛利率 41.6%，EPS 6.40 元。 
➢ 初次建議區間操作，目標價 424 元 
IET-KY 作為全球唯一一家採用 MBE 製程量產磊晶的供應商，客戶包含
多家全球雷射大廠。在產品規格上，公司已踏入 400G EAM 與 Driver、
200G VCSEL、QD Laser 等高規格產品所用之 Epi-Wafer 的研發，且
400G EAM 與 Driver、QD Laser 於 2027 年就會開始出貨，產品進程遠
遠領先同業。然而，在 400G 產品面臨龐大需求時，成本較低的 MOCVD
勢必取代初期採用的 MBE 成為主要技術。參考光通訊產業 Forward PE
評價多落在 20X~50X 區間，以 2028 年 EPS 換算，目前為 49X，評價
偏高。初次建議區間操作，目標價 424 元(2028F PE 52X)

4971 IET-KY 加權指數

### Page 2

- 2 -

個股報告

公司介紹 
英特磊科技股份有限公司(IET-KY)於 2011 年 4 月設立，為全球磷化銦(InP)、銻化鎵
(GaAs)、銻化鎵(GaSb)磊晶片代工領導廠，是少數採用 MBE 技術從事生產的磊晶供
應商，具有基板到磊晶的垂直整合技術能力，提供無線通訊、衛星通訊、光纖通訊等
商業用途，以及太空與國防上的紅外線感測、夜視、攝影等產品應用。3Q26 營收組
成為 GaAs 36.5%、InP 33.4%、GaSb 和勞務收入 26.5%與其它 3.5%。 
資料來源:國票投顧 
圖 1：IET-KY 營收組成

3Q26 出貨量回升，4Q26 認列設備營收，2026 年營收年增 17.4% 
由於 InP 基板供貨恢復穩定使 Epi-Wafer 出貨量成長，IET-KY 3Q26 營收 3.2 億元
(YoY+26.8%，QoQ+24.6%)。毛利率季增 4.7ppt 至 38.7%(YoY+7ppt)提升係因高
毛利水準之磊晶片及硬體銷售所帶動。EPS 1.26 元。3Q26 營收組成為 GaAs 36.5%、
InP 33.4%、GaSb 和勞務收入 26.5%與其它 3.5%。應用比重為射頻 19.6%、汽車防
撞及車用 13.3%、光纖網路 24.6%、高速傳輸元件 13.7%、紅外線偵測 25.2%、硬體
構件 3.6%。各項產品線營運狀況如下：(1)GaAs：射頻與國防應用合計占其比重約
20%，營收年增 72%。(2)InP：受惠於 5G 基礎建設、高頻光纖網路與雲端運算發展，
APD/PD 需求極為強勁，100G/200G PD 占整體營收 15%至 20%，但因基板供應受
限，營收年減 2%。(3)GaSb：由於國防客戶快速加單，商用訂單持續擴大，3Q26 營
收占比由 15%升至 26.5%，營收年增 18%。考量客製化修改特殊設備營收預計於 12
月認列，且基板供貨穩定，研究員認為 11 月與 10 月營收水準差異不大，12 月營收
高於 11 月，預計 4Q26 營收季增 12.4%至 3.6 億元(YoY+12.7%)，預估 2026 年營
收 12.7 億元(YoY+17.4%)，毛利率 40%，EPS 4.85 元。 
2027 年 m-PIN、400G EAM 與 Driver 開始量產，產能年增 10%至 20% 
展望 2027 年，各產品線發展如下：(1)GaAs：國防訂單與商用訂單同步增加 30%。
m-PIN(變晶型 PIN)可望進入量產階段。VCSEL 在新增機台後產能估約增加 40%。二
次長晶已由淡水廠接單，新機台即將完成驗收，已獲得固定客戶下單並進行認證，2027
年可支應客戶需求，預估 GaAs 營收年增 20%至 25%。(2)InP：第一位客戶已突破 InP

### Page 3

- 3 -

個股報告 
基板限制，預計 2027 年開始量產 400G EAM 與 Driver。在歐洲供應商導入，以及研
磨、晶片表面拋光重複使用等技術逐漸成熟下，若 2027 年基板供應能維持現有水準，
預期 InP Epi-Wafer 出貨量將有雙位數成長。(3)GaSb：公司著重於生物辨識等創新
應用，並已建置第二條 GaSb 生產線，預計於 2027 年正式量產。在公司新增機台後，
GaSb 產能估增加 30%。(4)勞務收入(硬體構件)：客製化新材料機台代營運及「由租
轉買」合約已接近完成。產能方面，在二期廠房擴建工程方面，經過審核與跨政黨行
政團隊過渡，法律與技術問題已全數解決，德州晶片法案補助款亦已陸續領取並支應
擴建工程，預計 4Q26 內完成簽署正式合約，1Q27 完工，可望於 2H27 開始挹注營
收。同時，配合晶片法案承諾，公司將於 2027 年購買 2 至 3 台機台，機台總數增至
18 台至 19 台，總產能約增加 10%至 20%。新增機台將優先配置於 VCSEL 與 GaSb。
假設工廠平均稼動率保持在 70%至 80%，與 2026 年相近，預估 2027 年營收 16.1
億元(YoY+27.1%)，毛利率在 400G EAM 與 Driver、VCSEL 等高毛利產品營收比重
上升下增至 41.6%，EPS 6.40 元。 
IET-KY 可望成為全球第三家 QD Laser Epi-Wafer 供應商 
展望 2028 年，IET-KY 於 2027 年進入試產階段的 QD Laser GaAs Epi-Wafer 可望開
始放量出貨，成為繼 QD Laser Inc.及 Innolume GmbH 之後，全球第三家 QD Laser 
Epi-Wafer 供應商。200G VCSEL 可望從研發階段步入試產或量產階段。在 InP 部分，
第二位 400G EAM 與 Driver 客戶產品將開始量產，與第一位客戶一同貢獻 InP 營收，
加上 1.6T 之 200G PD 出貨比重提升，預期 InP 營收仍會有雙位數成長。生物辨識所
需的 GaSb Epi-Wafer 則因市場持續擴大而出貨年增雙位數，預估 2028 年營收 19.3
億元(YoY+19.8%)，毛利率 42.4%，EPS 8.16 元。 
MBE 是下一代規格的起源，初次建議區間操作，目標價 424 元 
IET-KY 作為全球唯一一家採用 MBE 製程量產磊晶的供應商，客戶包含多家全球雷射
大廠。在產品規格上，已踏入 400G EAM 與 Driver、200G VCSEL、QD Laser 等高
規格產品所用之 Epi-Wafer 的研發，且 400G EAM 與 Driver、QD Laser 於 2027 年
就會開始出貨，產品進程遠遠領先同業。然而，在 400G 等產品面臨龐大需求時，成
本較低的 MOCVD 勢必取代初期採用的 MBE 成為主要技術，因此 IET-KY 也將維持
「營收規模小、毛利高」的路線，以前沿技術出貨高規格產品而穩定成長。參考光通
訊產業 Forward PE 評價多落在 20X~50X 區間，以 2028 年 EPS 換算，目前為 49X，
評價偏高。初次建議區間操作，目標價 424 元(2028F PE 52X)。

個股報告 
群聯(8299) 
AI 需求未減，營收規模持續擴大 
投資評等 目標價 
買進 $2150 
前次投資建議：2026.08.14： 
「買進」，目標價: $4000

報告日期：2026.11.09 研 究 員：王穎

摘 要 及 調 整 原 因 
➢ 看好 AI及企業級相關產品需求維持強勁，且 AI產品組合佔比將進一步擴大，帶動營收規模
擴大，維持「買進」投資評等，然低價庫存利益將逐季下滑導致毛利率下降，因此下修目
標價至 2150元(4 x 27(F)PER)，潛在上漲空間 43.3%。

基本資料 結論與建議 
2026/11/6 收盤價： 1,500.00 ➢ 26Q3 營收 860.76 億元，QoQ+26.8%，YoY+374.6%，
毛利率 59.9%，費用 184.89 億元，QoQ+5.25 億元，業
外獲利 30.77億元，稅後淨利 305.46億元，EPS 138元。
受惠產品組合轉向企業級及 AI 應用，使營收規模擴大，
然手機、PC 等消費性需求受到 NAND 漲價下出貨量下
滑，但群聯本身 controller 出貨量及營收仍提升，主要因
市占率提升，同時低價庫存利益將減少導致毛利率下滑。 
➢ 展望 26Q4 預期 NAND Falsh 報價持續提升且企業級及
AI相關之產品需求維持強勁，預期 AI Ecosystem module
將持續提升，預期營收續創新高，然庫存成本提高使毛利
率下滑。因此兆豐預估營收 991.6 億元，QoQ+15.2%，
YoY+334.9%，毛利率 56.4%，稅後淨利 308.22 億元，
EPS為 139.3元。 
➢ 展望 2026~2027 年，預期至 2027 仍呈現供不應求之態
勢，公司坐擁大量庫存，且群聯產品組合轉向 AI 
Ecosystem module 有助於有利營收規模持續放大，然隨
著庫存成本持續提升，預期毛利率將下滑。因此兆豐預估
營收 2940.9/4442.2 億元，YoY+304.7/51.1%，毛利率
60.1/51.7% ，稅後淨利 1027.6/1187.7 億元，
YoY+1075.8/15.6%，EPS 為 464.7/536.9元。 
➢ 群聯目前位於 2.8 X 27(F)PER)，維持「買進」投資評等，
下修目標價至 2150 元(4 x 27(F)PER)，潛在上漲空間
43.3%。

營 運 分 析

群聯為企業級 SSD領導廠商 
群聯電子股份有限公司成立於 2000年 11月 8 日，為國內 NAND FLASH控
制 IC 廠，主要業務為 NAND FLASH 控制 IC及周邊系統產品的研發設計製
造及銷售。公司已經成為 USB隨身碟、SD記憶卡、eMMC、UFS、PATA、
SATA 與 PCIe 固態磁碟等控制晶片領域的領頭廠。26Q3 產品組合 AI 
Ecosystem Module 57%、 Embedded ODM Module 24%、 Industrial 
Module9%、Controller 5%、Retail Module2%、Others 3%。

             群聯產品以 AI Ecosystem 相關產品為主

- 3 - 
個股報告 
26Q3產品組合轉佳帶動營收會模擴大 
26Q3 營收 860.76 億元，QoQ+26.8%，YoY+374.6%，毛利率 59.9%，費
用 184.89 億元，QoQ+5.25 億元，業外獲利 30.77 億元，稅後淨利 305.46
億元，EPS 138元。受惠產品組合轉向企業級及 AI應用，使營收規模擴大，
然手機、PC 等消費性需求受到 NAND 漲價下出貨量下滑，但群聯本身
controller 出貨量及營收仍提升，主要因市占率提升，預期待消費性及工業市
場歷經庫存調整後，有望逐步恢復。同時低價庫存利益將減少導致毛利率下
滑。26Q3 庫存金額 1117.7 億元，較 26Q2 增加 199.8 億元，存貨週轉天數
由 297天下降至 274天，存貨主要配置於企業級、AI應用及 boot drive 等應
用，以因應後續客戶需求。

26Q4預期營收規模擴大獲利持續創高 
展望 26Q4預期 NAND Falsh報價持續提升，於 main drive持續取得不同客
戶 design win，main drive對於 NAND容量需求更大，於 26Q3取得多項新
專案，預期 26Q4~27Q1將持續增加。同時 PCIe Gen6企業級 SSD預期 2026
年 11 月底開始提供樣品，與 CPU 業者共同發布相關 solution，主要鎖定 AI
推論相關應用，因此企業級及 AI相關之產品需求將於 2027年維持強勁，預
期 AI Ecosystem module 將持續提升，預期營收續創新高，然庫存成本提高
使毛利率下滑。因此兆豐預估營收 991.6億元，QoQ+15.2%，YoY+334.9%，
毛利率 56.4%，稅後淨利 308.22億元，EPS 為 139.3元。

圖二、2026營運預期逐季增溫  
群聯各季營收及獲利走勢預估(單位：百萬)

個股報告 
股利配發創下新高 
26H1 EPS 達 187.4元，董事會決議通過上半年度盈餘分配現金股利案，每
股擬配發 60 元現金股利，創下歷史新高，配發率約 32%，半年殖利率約
2.63%。若 26H2獲利及現金流更佳，2027年 1月可再評估提高配發比例，
全年殖利率目標有望達 5%。 
圖三、NAND歷年供需比

資料來源：兆豐投顧整理

2026 NAND Flash 供需缺口逐步擴大 
NAND FLASH市場呈現明顯的週期性循環，每約 3.5～4年進入一個完整供
需週期，2026 NAND Flash 呈現供不應求，且下半年供需缺口擴大，有利報
價穩定甚至持續上漲。同時 Nvidia ICMS (Inference Context Memory 
Storage) 的快取層級架構，用於解決大型語言模型推論時的記憶體瓶頸，推
升 NAND需求，提升 Token吞吐量 5 倍、推論延遲顯著下降、能效優化 5 倍。
預期 2027年用於 Nvidia KV Cache 之 NAND 需求將達總需求約 10%。進一
步造成供需缺口擴大。 
圖四、Nvidia ICMS

資料來源：兆豐投顧整理

- 5 - 
個股報告 
預期 AI 佔比提升帶動營收規模放大 
2026~2027 年，預期至 2027 仍呈現供不應求之態勢，公司坐擁大量庫存，
main drive、PCIe Gen6等產品預期至 2027年都將持續成長使群聯產品組合
轉向 AI Ecosystem module 有助於有利營收規模持續放大，另外在消費性市
場持續耕耘，取得更高手機、PC市占率，Client SSD方面，2026年已取得
6 個 PCIe Gen5 TLC 及 QlC controller 專案，未來將協助原廠開發相容
aiDAPTIV之 Client SSD方案，結合 AI PC於記憶體擴充需求，擴大 PC市
場影響力，因此出貨量將持續增加，待消費性庫存歷經調整後，出貨量將進
一步提升，然隨著庫存成本持續提升，預期毛利率將下滑。因此兆豐預估營
收 2940.9/4442.2 億元，YoY+304.7/51.1%，毛利率 60.1/51.7%，稅後淨利
1027.6/1187.7 億元，YoY+1075.8/15.6%，EPS為 464.7/536.9元。

看好 AI 相關產品組合佔比提升，維持「買進」投資評等 
群聯目前位於 2.8 X 27(F)PER)，看好 AI及企業級相關產品需求維持強勁，
且 AI產品組合佔比將進一步擴大，維持「買進」投資評等，然低價庫存利益
將逐季下滑導致毛利率下降，因此下修目標價至 2150元(4 x 27(F)PER)，潛
在上漲空間 43.3%。

## Document 314: Cliff Notes_ cost of living front and centre (2026-11-17)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Cliff Notes_ cost of living front and centre (2026-11-17).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-17
Pages: 11
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: cost of living front and centre
By Ryan Wells, Elliot Clarke & Illiana Jain
09:49 November 13 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
In Australia, the Westpac-MI Consumer Sentiment Index fell 2.7% to 78.2 in November, its weakest
reading since late-2022 and a level historically associated with periods of severe economic stress.
Renewed cost-of-living and interest rate pressures were front and centre following another lift in

inflation, including sharply higher petrol prices, and the RBA’s November rate increase. Mortgage
holders are feeling the squeeze most acutely, with sentiment across this group falling to a record low.

### Page 2

Consumers have become noticeably more downbeat on the near-term economic outlook – the relevant
sub-index dropping 10.2% to be 22% below its long-run average. Job-loss fears also continue to edge
up.
Businesses are similarly troubled. The latest NAB business survey showed confidence slipped further
to –15 in October as conditions held near their weakest level since the pandemic. Of concern for the
outlook, forward orders fell to –12, also the weakest read since the pandemic, and capital expenditure

intentions deteriorated. Purchase costs continue to grow materially faster than selling prices, further
compressing already tight margins.
Housing provides a clear example of how caution and tighter financial conditions can jolt activity. The
value of new dwelling finance approvals plunged 15% in Q3, the largest quarterly decline in the series’
history, taking approvals 25% lower year-to-date. Investor lending bore the brunt of the adjustment,
falling 30% over the quarter as higher rates combined with the housing tax changes announced in May.

Owner-occupier lending also weakened, however, with first-home buyers proving particularly sensitive
to the deterioration in affordability and market conditions.
Taken together, the data points to a further loss of momentum across the domestic economy,
consistent with Westpac’s expectation for subdued growth and concern over downside activity risks.
Meanwhile, in the US, October’s headline CPI met our expectations but at 0.4% was a touch above the

consensus view. Energy and food both contributed meaningfully to the result, but core inflation was
also marginally stronger than forecast, +0.3%. Core goods inflation has accelerated over the past four
months to average a gain of 0.2% as businesses look to pass tariffs, tech and energy input cost
inflation through. Shelter meanwhile continued to rise at an above-average pace, gaining 0.3% in
October.
The market impact of the October CPI report was accentuated by hawkish remarks from Fed Governor

Waller prior to the release. He expressed confidence in economic activity and the labour market while
showing significant concern over the strength and persistence of inflation, focusing on the passthrough
of energy and technology input inflation. His conclusion for policy was clear: "if, as I expect, the
monthly rate of inflation improves in October, I am leaning toward supporting an increase in our policy
rate of 25 basis points at the FOMC's December meeting"; but "if the October CPI inflation data out

later today are similar to the past two monthly readings—with core CPI inflation around 0.3 percent or
even higher—then a stronger policy move may be needed".
Regional Fed Presidents Goolsbee, Logan and Hammack also kept the focus on inflation risks and
stated or implied the FOMC needs to act. Market participants have responded to this week’s updates,
pricing a 25bp hike in for the December and January meetings, and a cumulative 3.5 hikes by
September. We believe this pricing overstates the need for policy tightening given core inflation ex

shelter remains relatively benign at 2.4% on a six-month annualised basis and the labour market is
balanced; but it will take time for price concerns to subside.
While we agree that the US economy is strong in aggregate, US households are under pressure from
the cost of living, and their investment decision making is constrained by borrowing costs. Highlighting

### Page 3

Related articles
this, US existing home sales fell 5.5% in October after a downwardly revised 1.0% gain in September,
leaving the level of sales below the cycle lows of 2009 and 2010. Our November Market Outlook will
provide a full update on our expectations for the US and the world when released on Westpac IQ this
afternoon.

6
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.41 / 9 November 2026ECONEWS
Find out more in our scenario and forecasts
ADVANCED ECONOMIES
In brief. The week was dominated by large market swings triggered by
the US midterms results. In the end, energy prices end the week much
lower, bond yields somewhat lower and stock prices down in tech-heavy
markets and slightly up elsewhere, notably in Europe. Incoming data
confirmed a picture of resilient growth overall. The Bank of England

hiked its policy rate by 25 bps and signaled it could do more. Market
pressure on France eased somewhat.
EUROZONE
Energy pushes inflation up, but underlying pressures remain rela -
tively contained. Headline inflation rose to 4.0% y/y in October, its
highest in three years, strengthening the case for another ECB rate
increase in December. Energy remained the main driver, while core in-
flation rose by 2.6%. The ECB wage tracker points to negotiated wage

growth slowing to 2.3% by September 2027. Meanwhile, retail sales fell
0.7% m/m in September and the average interest rate on new business
loans increased 13bp to 3.90%.
To watch next week: September trade balance, Q3 GDP second esti -
mate and employment (Friday).
France
EVs boost activity, market pressure eases somewhat. After already
bolstering household consumption in Q3, new car registrations rose
7% y/y in October (+4% y/y YTD), driven by another surge in electric

car sales (+81% y/y in October; +78% y/y YTD). In parallel, manufac -
turing output rebounded by 0.5% m/m in September, supported by
the automotive sector (+7.5% m/m but -5.5% y/y) and capital goods
(+2.6% m/m and +4.8% y/y). However, industrial production fell by
0.5% m/m (and 1.3% y/y), as a result of a decline in electricity produc -
tion (-4.2% m/m). The composite October PMI was revised down by
1.8 points from the flash estimate to 46.6 (-4.5 m/m), dragged down by

services. This sharp downgrade likely reflects the hit to sentiment from
the October 17 riots; however, precedents suggest activity itself should
not be visibly impacted. Appropriately indifferent to the meandering
journey of the draft budget through Parliament, the OAT-bund spread
narrowed to 155 bps (-10 bps in one week) amid a broad-based decline
in government bond yields across advanced economies; Thursday’s
long-term debt auction, the last of the year, was well-subscribed. Stri-

ving to meet its deficit target of 5.4% of GDP in 2026, the government
announced EUR3.8bn of spending cuts to offset EUR3.7bn in additional
expenditure. The High Council for Public Finance nevertheless sees the
deficit reaching 5.5%. The 2027 budget will start being discussed by
the Senate from next week, in its original version, after the national
Assembly unanimously rejected its amended version that would have
led to a 5.9% deficit.
To watch next week: Q3 unemployment rate and the Banque de France

monthly business survey (Tuesday).
Germany
Industrial activity picks up. New orders rebounded 4.4% m/m in Sep -
tember and industrial production increased 0.7%, with the auto sec -
tor delivering particularly striking gains of 10.5% in orders and 17.1%
in production. Mechanical-engineering orders also recovered (+8.3%),
while pharmaceutical production increased by 8.0%. By contrast, large
orders in aerospace, shipbuilding, rail and defence fell by 10.4%, while

machinery and equipment production declined by 6.5%. October's com-
posite PMI was revised up to 53.4, signaling continued strong expan -
sion.
Italy
Services keep the economy above water as manufacturing slips back.
The composite PMI eased to 50.8 in October, its lowest since May.
Manufacturing returned to contraction at 49.4, its weakest reading
since January, while services strengthened to 52.0 and recorded a fifth
consecutive month of growth. Both sectors reported increasing input-

cost and output-price inflation.
UNITED KINGDOM
The BoE hikes and leaves the door open to more. The Bank of England
raised Bank Rate by 25bp to 4%, its first increase since August 2023, in a
more hawkish than expected 8-1 vote, with Catherine Mann favouring
a 50bp hike. The November Monetary Policy Report sees CPI inflation
peaking around 5% y/y by mid-2027 and upgraded Q4 2026 growth to
2.2%, while cutting the 2027 and 2028 growth projections. The com -

posite PMI meanwhile improved to 52.9, its highest since February,
with new business growing at its strongest pace in nine months and
private-sector employment increasing for the first time in 25 months.
To watch next week: Q3 GDP, business investment, industrial produc -
tion (Thursday).
UNITED STATES
Post election market jitters and a hot jobs report. The Democrats’
unexpectedly sweeping victory in the midterms (the largest wave
since 1974) initially led markets to rally on (i) relief that the President

recognised the results, and (ii) hopes of a swift end to the Iran war
(oil prices and bond yields down, stock prices up). But this was soon
followed by a sharp equity sell-off on 5 November, with the S&P 500
down 4.4% and the Nasdaq down 5.0%, erasing USD2.7 Trillion of mar-
ket capitalization in one day. Why? Because of the realization that vo -
ters strongly supported AI-restrictive measures on the ballot (46 out
of 47) and candidates — for Congress and Governor seats — in favour

of curtailing data centers buildout. By the week-end, however, senti -
ment had turned around again, helped in part by a much stronger than
expected jobs report: payrolls increased by 165,000 in October against
a 70,000 consensus, while the previous two months were revised hi -
gher and unemployment edged down to 4.1%. Average hourly earnings
rose 0.4% m/m, their strongest monthly pace since November 2025.
Manufacturing activity also strengthened, with the ISM rising to 55.5,

and the non-manufacturing index easing to a still buoyant 54.5.
To watch next week: October CPI (Tuesday); October PPI (Friday).
JAPAN
Wage growth accelerates. Scheduled contractual earnings increased
3.7% y/y in September and total wage growth accelerated to 4.6%,
helped by strong bonus payments. Real wage growth remained posi -
tive at 2.2% y/y.
EMERGING ECONOMIES
Portfolio outflows. According to IIF data, non-resident portfolio invest-
ment flows recorded USD33.8bn of outflows in October, the largest

monthly outflow since March and up from USD19.3bn in September.
Equities accounted for USD31.7bn, led by USD28.3bn from emerging
Asia, as doubts over AI-investment returns and profit-taking hit North
Asian markets particularly hard. Taiwan and South Korea suffered a
further sharp market correction in the first week of November.

### Page 2

7
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.41 / 9 November 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
Find out more in our scenario and forecasts
ASIA
PMI: expanding except for the Philippines. The ASEAN PMI rose to 53.1
in October from 52.1, pointing to faster expansion. India remained ro -
bust despite a slight decline (54.7). In Taiwan and South Korea, the
manufacturing PMIs declined but remained expansionary, at 54.9 and
51.3, respectively. Indonesia’s real GDP growth accelerated to 5.5% y/y

in Q3 2026 from 5.3% in Q2 marking its fastest pace since 2013.
China
The PBOC steps in as equity correction deepens. The central bank in -
creased its use of liquidity tools designed to support equity purchases,
including the special relending facility for share buybacks and stake in-
creases and a third round of the Securities, Funds and Insurance Com-
panies Swap Facility, for which 31 institutions applied for more than
RMB100bn. The measures follow heavy stock-market losses between

June and October. The PBOC also raised the reserve-requirement ratio
on foreign-currency deposits to 6% from 4%, effective 15 November, in
an effort to slow the renminbi's appreciation.
Weak domestic inflation pressure: In October, CPI inflation slowed to
0.7% y/y from 0.9% in September. Fuel prices increased by 20.2% y/y,
food prices fell -2.9% and core inflation slowed to 0.8% y/y from 1.0%.
Producer price inflation continued to rise (reaching 5.7% vs. 5.0% in

September), principally driven by higher energy and electronics prices.
To watch next week: External-trade data (Tuesday).
EMEA
Central Europe
PMIs expansionary except in Romania. Poland’s PMI hit the 50-mark
for the first time since April 2025 thanks to export orders. PMI was
also higher in Czech Rep. (55), supported by new orders, output and
employment. Hungary’s PMI fell marginally to 53.1.
Poland and Czech Republic central banks hold and hike respectively,

as expected.
Gulf
PMIs resilient except Qatar (deteriorating further to 46.3).
Saudi Arabia: Massive budget deficit in sight. Saudi Arabia recorded a
budget deficit of USD22.5bn in Q3, narrowing by 5% y/y. But over the
first nine months of 2026, budget deficit reached USD65.1bn, up from
USD48bn in 2025, despite a rather good revenue performance (+2.8%
total; +7.8% for non-oil revenues; -1.3% for oil revenues). The 8% rise in
spending in Q1-Q3 partly due to the war will make it difficult to meet

the 2026 revised target (deficit of 4.9% of GDP).
LATIN AMERICA
PMIs remain weak across the region, with only Colombia in expansion.
Brazil’s central bank pauses rate cuts, whereas Mexico’s central bank
cuts rates whilst signalling an upcoming pause. On 4 November, Bra -
zil’s central bank held its Selic rate at 13.75%, interrupting a series of
five consecutive cuts, amid rising inflation (4.6% y/y in September, up
from 4.2%), a sharp post-election real depreciation on fiscal concerns,

and higher energy prices. The central bank also revised up its 2027
inflation forecast to 4.7% (from 3.9%) and removed forward guidance
hinting at further cuts. Mexico’s central bank unexpectedly cut its po -
licy rate by 25bp to 6.25% on 5 November. This decision came despite
above-target inflation (3.5% against a 3% target), a peso depreciation
of more than 9% against USD since September, and relatively strong Q3
GDP growth (3.2% y/y). The central bank signaled that rates would now

remain on hold for an extended period.
COMMODITIES
Geopolitical relief delivers a dramatic energy-price reversal. Mar -
kets have priced in the reduced risk of short-term conflict escalation
through a sharp decline in prices. Beyond this, however, they lack di -
rection, wavering between positive signals (the resumption of indirect
negotiations between the belligerents) and less favorable ones (in -
creased vessel damage in the strait). Brent dropped 7.6% over the week

to USD96.8/bbl as markets priced a reduced risk of near-term conflict
escalation. European gas prices fell almost in lockstep, declining 7.7%
to EUR87.6/MWh. US commercial crude inventories continued to de -
cline, although more modestly than over the preceding four weeks.
Diesel crack spread’s reaction to US elections has been much more
limited than for crude. EU gas storage ended the refilling season at 75%
of capacity (vs. 80% goal).
y/y = year-on-year

m/m = month-on-month
bp = basis point
pp = percentage point
mb = million barrels
MBtu = Million British Thermal Units

## Document 342: Eurozone_ Improving business sentiment, driven by tech, is spreading to other sectors (2026-10-14)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Eurozone_ Improving business sentiment, driven by tech, is spreading to other sectors (2026-10-14).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-14
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

14 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
EUROZONE: IMPROVING BUSINESS SENTIMENT, DRIVEN BY TECH, IS SPREADING TO OTHER SECTORS
Stéphane Colliac & Tarik Rharrab
*LATEST OBSERVATION: 5 OCTOBER 2026  **ESTIMATE
The September PMI surveys confirm that growth in the Eurozone is getting stronger, boosted in particular by new export orders, which are
still rising. Sectors that were already performing well – technology and capital goods (including for defence) – are continuing to grow, while

those that had been struggling until now – aeronautics, consumer goods and financial services – are seeing their production indices recover.
This strong performance is particularly noteworthy, given that it has not yet been undermined by higher inflation.
The heatmap shows the PMI index readings: a reading above 50 indicates expansion, while a reading below 50 signals contraction compared to the previous
month. For each indicator, sectors are ranked in descending order based on their September 2026 reading.

Shades of red represent values above 50, while shades of blue represent values below 50: the intensity of the colour increases the further away they are
from this threshold.
Production growth spreads to more sectors
The PMI surveys on business sentiment confirm that business activity in the Eurozone is expanding at a faster pace and spreading to more sectors.
Household-consumption-related sectors (goods and services), which were adversely affected in the spring by the outbreak of the conflict in the Middle

East, do not appear to be negatively impacted by the rising inflation observed since August. The aeronautics sector, where production has mainly been
limited by supply constraints, is also showing signs of improvement.

Chart of the Week / 14 October 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
These trends, although still moderate, come alongside a drive to upgrade equipment, fuelled by investment in digital technology, electrification
(which is supporting the automotive sector) and defence. The momentum in technology goods and services further strengthened in September.
The production index for computer and electronics hit a five-year high, while the software services production index stayed close to its July peak.

Financial services are also enjoying a significant improvement, reflecting the healthy lending momentum despite interest rates starting to rise
(see our analysis).
Growth driven by exports
Exports drove growth in the first half of 2026, both across the Eurozone as a whole (see our analysis) and among its key members (notably
Germany). This momentum is also increasing, as indicated by the ‘new export orders’ component of the various PMI indices. As was the case with

production, there has been a particularly noteworthy increase for capital goods (technology and machinery) and related services, particularly digital
services. This trend is playing out more broadly, as, in September, new export orders rose across all sub-sectors, with the exception of intermediate
goods and consumer goods, where contraction recently eased. There has been a significant improvement in the transport sector, particularly in
the aeronautics industry, which has major markets in Asia. Recent data on French exports show that aeronautics exports to this region are rising

once again.
The convergence between rising production and increasing export-order backlogs shows that Eurozone growth is based on an increasingly solid
foundation. Inflationary pressures are not holding it back for the time being.

## Document 407: Sales Trader Commentary_ Squeezing the Shorts, Fading the Rallies; Navigating TAIEX Chaos Before Heading to the Vice City _ GSTW

Source PDF: all report pdf/Sales Trader Commentary_ Squeezing the Shorts, Fading the Rallies; Navigating TAIEX Chaos Before Heading to the Vice City _ GSTW.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-11
Pages: 11
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Sales Trader Commentary: Squeezing the
Shorts, Fading the Rallies; Navigating TAIEX
Chaos Before Heading to the Vice City | GSTW
12 November 2026 | 3:15 PM Taiwan Standard Time
Robert Wang
Goldman Sachs (Asia) L.L.C. Taipei Branch
FICC & Equities
Post the historical open on 11/6/2026, we saw a jaw-dropping 14% climb from day-
low last Friday. Finally took a breather (and a big one too) today; admittedly, I was
writing a different narrative, but last hour fade in Taiwan has forced me to rewrite this

(>1k points drop and picked up market turnover). So, where do we go from here?
Check out below for my observation and investor feedback.
Key Investor Feedback and Takeaway
Sentiment. Sentiment is still cautious among foreigners; a lot of HFs faced serious
short squeeze from this rally. Yet, many are still reluctant to cover all their short
positions given the new short sell restrictions. and hence held on to the pain trades.
Interestingly, market still exhaust short sell quota almost every day. Additionally,

there is little conviction for a full-on rally and also low willingness to chase
around here given year-end. Overall, HFs have de-grossed much, one of the most
aggressive de-gross per GS prime book data. Key focus will be whether LOs are
willing to participate and to what extent will they add back the positions. Only
then, we will likely see a more sustainable rally further from here. Bottom line: are
we out of the woods? Not yet. Cautious optimism is perhaps the most positive

thing I can say for now.
Positioning. Foreigners have net sold for 10 straight sessions, totaling around
US$25.4bn. Most outflows are in TSMC but worth noting that we finally saw TSMC
net buy for the first time since 2 weeks yesterday. On the other hand, local funds
have been good size net buyers to absorb the foreign outflows, mainly from retail's
mutual fund subscription. Just regurgitating some of my berg blurbs from the past
few days, we still see lots of short interest in crowded long sectors, most in

ABF/CCL (i.e. NY Plastics) followed by CPO names; and most smid cap
outperformance was driven by short covering. The overly sold names saw little
attention and minimum desire to bottom fish (i.e. Delta, Chroma, KYEC). On a
brighter note, we also picked up some real money inflows into TSMC this week, but
I don't know whether it's enough to turn the tide. The more convicted long right
now for foreigners is still ASE in my opinion.
Retail. This is perhaps the most important factor now in Taiwan. Coupled with the

sentiment boost from National Stabilization Fund, many retail decided to hold
instead of cutting loss and even bought into the dip. For those retail that were
facing margin call during this correction, some were eventually saved by the market
rally. Cleaner positioning for sure, though perhaps not clean enough to fuel another
11/12/26, 3:20 PM Sales Trader Commentary: Squeezing the Shorts, Fading the Rallies; Navigating TAIEX Chaos Before Heading to the Vice City | GSTW

https://marquee.gs.com/s/content/markets/dc88e4dd-47de-45c1-bbf3-31c2c5c57299 1/11

### Page 2

10% climb in index. Per my local investor feedback, the smart pundits actually
many have missed out on this rally (of course they've also de-grossed much ahead).
Margin loan balance and maintenance ratio are two key data that I will follow
closely in the coming weeks.
Fundamentals. Fundamentals are still good with most company reporting record
high quarterly earnings. Monthly revenue also grew MoM. But stock trading is all
about forward looking. People are anticipating AI capex growth to slow down or

even plateau in the coming 2 years. Quanta (2382 TT) earnings call yesterday
validated this narrative - still high growth in 2027 but unlikely to see another triple-
digits rapid growth like 2026. Earnings outlooks probably matter less than
positioning and macro backdrop. With that, we will conclude most of our 3Q26
earnings this week with Hon Hai (2317 TT) as the main finale.
Liquidity Event: MSCI just published their quarterly review, and NYPCB (8046 TT)

and TUC (6274 TT) will be added into the index, while Lotes (3533 TT), Chailease
(5871 TT), Wan Hai (2615 TT), an E-Ink (8069 TT) will be removed. Kinsus (3189
TT) did not get added due to its disposition status. See below and this report for
our flows estimate.
Risk. Risk reward level here is not attractive, especially when the rebound came so
aggressively. Momentum to sustain the rally is not constructive, so investors should
be cautious of more volatility and further unwind (if there's anything to be

unwound). Jensen and Nvidia's headlines are certainly not helping, though I did see
some funny memes on the social media. Credit is another risk here; neoclouds top
line is +ve but let's not forget at what cost are these companies carving those
revenue from. Otherwise, market seems to process the macro prints well, with now
expecting a rate hike in Dec'26 and Mar'27. Last but not least, cross-strait tension
can be a focus next during Trump-Xi meeting at the APEC Summit next week. I

know the Taiwanese has been (probably will be) indifferent and just another BAU,
but anything assertively negative comment on Taiwan might fret the macro
traders, such as Xi's comment just now.
My View. Not a BEAR so far. I do not think the market is broken, but I
acknowledge the downside risk has risen again and enlarged as I respect the
technicals. That said, I am still excited about next week, even though we have fewer
fundamental catalysts now. In the US, Nvidia is set to report next week. A lighter

book might fare better in today's environment. To end on a fun personal note, GTA
VI will finally come out after 13 years of waiting. So if you can't find me on
Bloomberg, try looking for me in the Vice City.

## Document 411: September CPI_ Inflation accelerating in the run up to Christmas (2026-11-17)

Source PDF: all report pdf/September CPI_ Inflation accelerating in the run up to Christmas (2026-11-17).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-17
Pages: 18
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#inflation #australia #rba #economicbulletinsaustralia #inflation
September CPI: Inflation accelerating in the run up to Christmas
By Justin Smirk & Neha Sharma
16:08 October 28 2026 Share
September Monthly CPI: Headline 0.8%mth/4.3%yr, Trimmed Mean 0.3.%mth/3.6%yr.
Market services ex volatile 1.6%mth/4.0%yr. September Quarter CPI: Headline
1.3%qtr/3.9%yr; Trimmed Mean 1.0%qtr/3.7%yr.
The CPI rose 0.8% in the September month, stronger than Westpac’s 0.6% estimate and the

market’s expectation of 0.7%. The annual pace picked up from 4.0%yr to 4.3%yr, The seasonally
adjusted CPI rose 0.6% in September highlighting some positive seasonality in the month.

### Page 2

Key factors driving the headline monthly result were: automotive fuels (9.4%mth); domestic holiday
travel & accommodation (8.0%mth); international holiday travel & accommodation (7.2%mth); and
property rates & charges (5.5%mth). There were modest offsets from: women’s garments (–
3.1%mth); household textiles (–6.6%mth); and accessories (–4.1%mth).
The Trimmed Mean (TM) gained 0.3% in September, spot on both Westpac and market expectations
with the annual pace lifting from 3.5%yr (revised from 3.6%yr) to 3.6%yr. The six-month annualised

pace is now 4.0%yr the fastest in the short history of this series.
Headline CPI lifted 1.3% in the September quarter compared to an expectation of 1.3% by Westpac
and 1.2% by the market. This saw the annual pace hold at 3.9%yr.
The RBA’s key underlying inflation measure, the quarterly TM, lifted 1.0% vs an expectation of
0.9% by Westpac and market estimate of 1.0%. The annual pace lifted from 3.6%yr to 3.7%yr
with the six-month annualised pace accelerating to 3.7%yr from 3.4%yr.

Overall, the estimates come in broadly as expected so we see minimal risk to our current December
quarter forecast of 1.3%qtr for the headline CPI. Softer housing inflation does present a very modest
downside risk to our 0.9%qtr for the TM but there are offsetting gains elsewhere.
The September CPI update revealed underlying inflationary pressures continuing even while a
moderation in housing inflation (dwellings in particular) is unfolding slightly faster than we had

anticipated. As such, the near-term risk for inflation remains to the upside. The RBA will remain alert
to these near-term inflationary risks and as so, we hold to the view that the RBA will raise rates again
at the November meeting.

### Page 3

Month of September CPI
The monthly CPI rose 0.8%mth in September, above both our expectation of a 0.6%mth increase and
the market forecast of 0.7%mth. This lifted the annual pace by 0.3ppts to 4.3%yr. On a seasonally
adjusted basis, the CPI increased 0.6%mth.
The key contributors to the monthly increase were: automotive fuel (9.4%mth, +0.3ppts); and holiday
travel & accommodation (7.6%mth, +0.5ppts), alongside a smaller contribution from the annual reset in

property rates (5.5%mth, +0.1ppt). There was no single major offset. Instead, several components
across household furnishings and clothing & footwear recorded modest declines, together subtracting a
little over 0.1ppt from the monthly result.
The monthly trimmed mean (TM) rose 0.3%mth, in line with both our and the market’s expectations.
The annual pace edged up to 3.6%yr, although the August outcome was revised down to 3.5%yr.
Underlying inflation has remained broadly stuck in the 3.5–3.6%yr range since May. On a 3m/3m basis,

the monthly TM was 1.1%.

### Page 4

Market services excluding volatile items and holiday travel were flat in the month, with annual inflation
easing to 3.8%yr, its softest pace since November 2025. On a seasonally adjusted basis, monthly
inflation moderated across most components in this measure, with telecommunications services, rents
and other financial services the key exceptions. However, all but two components are still recording
annual price growth above 3%, suggesting that disinflation across market services remains slow and

relatively narrow. The persistence of these underlying price pressures is likely to remain a concern for
the RBA.

### Page 5

Housing inflation was notably softer in the month, rising 0.3%mth to be 5.2% higher over the year.
Rents increased 0.2%mth, slightly below our expectation for a 0.3%mth rise. The biannual indexation
of Commonwealth Rent Assistance occurred late in the month, weighing on the September outcome,
with the remainder of the impact expected to flow through in October. The ABS noted that rents would
have risen 0.3%mth in the absence of the measure, while on a seasonally adjusted basis rents

increased 0.4%mth.
New dwelling inflation was flat in the month, the softest reading since May 2025. Monthly inflation has
now slowed in each successive month since May 2026, suggesting homebuilders’ pricing power is
becoming increasingly constrained. The ABS noted that some project home builders raised base prices
this month only to be offset by some builders increasing discounts and promotional offers in response
to slowing demand. The annual pace eased to 4.7%yr. Dwelling repair costs were also a downside

surprise, falling 0.1%mth, their first decline since February 2025, although prices remain 3.4% higher
over the year. Some of this softness was offset by a firmer-than-expected increase in property rates
and charges, which rose 5.6%mth.
Recently, we have also focused on audio, visual and computing given the potential for spillovers from
strong global demand for semiconductors and other computing resources associated with the AI build
out. Prices for both equipment and services came in weaker than August, with equipment prices falling

1.1%mth, while services rose 0.3%mth. Despite this, annual price growth remains relatively high for
these categories, running at 4.6% and 4.9% respectively. Some of this pressure might also be showing

### Page 6

through in gaming, toys and hobbies, which is running at 5.2%mth/15.5%yr.
Overall, the breadth of inflation moderated for a second consecutive month. The share of the basket
running above 3%yr fell from 40% to 35%, meanwhile the share below 2%yr lifted from 27% to 32%.
Our replication of the San Francisco Fed’s inflation shock momentum served as a good guide to the
quarterly TM. With the full monthly reads in for the September quarter in, this measure does signal
some persistence/upside pressures for the quarterly TM going into the coming quarters. It is worth
cautioning that this measure will be impacted as the ABS refines its seasonal adjustment process for
the monthly indexes.

Compared to our expectations, the key surprises were:
Upside surprises
Downside surprises
Fruit & vegetables was the key upside surprise, 0.6%mth against our expectations for 3.7%mth fall.
Holiday travel & accommodation also surprised to the upside, following a run of downside surprises
since early this year. It rose 7.6%mth against our expectations for a 4.4%mth lift. 
Automotive fuel rose 9.4%mth against expectations of an 8.3%mth lift.

### Page 7

September Quarter CPI
Clothing & footwear was the key downside surprise with prices falling 2.7%mth against our
expectations for a 0.2%mth fall. The misses were broad-based: garments (–2.3%mth vs –2.0%mth);
footwear (–2.5%mth vs 1.8%mth); and accessories & clothing services (–4.1%mth vs 2.8%mth).
Household textiles declined 6.6%mth against our expectations for a 3.2%mth fall. Furniture &
furnishings rose by less-than-expected 0.4%mth vs 1.6%mth. Household appliances, utensils and

tools also came in softer, –3.2%mth vs –2.4%mth.
Dairy prices fell 1.2%mth against our expectations for a 0.1%mth lift.

### Page 8

The September quarter CPI printed spot on our expectation of 1.3%. The key factor driving this
increase was the slightly stronger surge in auto fuel (7.8%, +0.27ppt) and holiday travel &
accommodation (2.7%qtr, +0.16ppt) being offset by a slightly softer than expected increase in housing
(1.1%qtr, +0.24ppt) with rents and dwellings both increasing 1.0% in the quarter and utilities lifting
1.2%. Food was also slightly stronger than expected (0.7%qtr, +0.12ppt) and while not significant to the
overall result it is interesting to track the ongoing rise in motor vehicle prices (0.3%qtr) despite a very
competitive market with Chinese manufacturers rapidly gaining market share over legacy brands.

It is worth noting that food was stronger than we expected (0.7% vs 0.5%) with a robust 1.7% increase
in restaurant meals and a solid 1.2% increase in take away & fast foods outweighing the –0.4% decline
in fresh fruit & vegetables and meat & seafoods. Overall, market services ex volatile prices increased a
very solid 1.2%qtr but with the annual pace easing to 3.2%yr from 3.36%yr.
Outside of housing the other main downside surprise in the quarter was the –0.6% fall in clothing &
footwear (+0.3% expected) with prices falling across just about all categories of garments, footwear
and accessories.
September Quarter Trimmed Mean

### Page 9

Recall that the TM is calculated from seasonally adjust consumption expenditure classes so any
numbers quoted here may not be the same as the original estimates quoted from the CPI. The TM gain
of 1.0% in the quarter was slightly stronger than we had expected even though dwellings (which have a
weight of almost 11% in the TM) was a touch softer at 1.0% compared to the 1.1% we had pencilled in.
Rents (1.0%, 10% weight) also made a solid contribution as did restaurant meals (1.7%qr, 6% weight),

take away & fast food (1.2%, 4% weight), other financial service (1.0%, 6% weight). Domestic travel
was in the TM (1.0%, 4% weight) as was medical & hospital service (1.0%, weight 5%). The range of
the TM was from 0.0% (motor vehicles) to 1.7% (beer).
What does this mean for our December quarter forecast and into 2027?
We are working though the monthly results to see what changes they may imply for individual
component inflationary momentum. Our preliminary analysis suggests that the softer trend in dwellings

is not enough, on its own, to change our Q4 CPI forecast of 1.3%. It does, however, suggest that if all
else held equal (which is not the case as there is likely to be some offsetting stronger gains elsewhere)
the moderation in dwelling inflation presents a very modest downside risk our Q4 TM estimate of 0.9%.
We are still working the numbers and will publish an updated inflation profile as soon as we can. In
addition, the overall momentum in underlying inflationary pressures suggest the downside risk to the

TM from dwellings is more likely to appear in 2027 than in the last quarter of 2026.

Marion Laboure | Camilla Siazon |  STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?  |  October  20, 2026
STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?
Institutional adoption of stablecoins continues to incrementally progress in 2026. While
the frenzy from GENIUS has diminished, stablecoins’ links to the financial system have
continued to deepen, and more policymakers are seriously considering their use cases.
The market cap has hovered around ~$300bn over the past 12 months, a sign that the

stablecoin market is maturing. Stablecoin payments, while still marginal relative to
stablecoins' crypto-native purpose, are also growing, rising from around $103bn in
adjusted transaction volume in 2022 to $375bn in 2026 YTD.
For now, institutional adoption remains concentrated in cross-border payments, treasury
management, 24/7 liquidity provision, collateral, and real-time settlement.
The next big question is whether stablecoins will evolve to become merely a payments

technology, or a genuine disruptor to the global banking system. As we previously
established, the US promotion of stablecoins remains a geopolitical tool to ensure USD
dominance. Bloomberg also reported that the Trump administration is considering joint
ventures with private actors to promote USD stablecoins overseas, in a bid to boost the
dollar’s dominance and source demand for US Treasuries. Last week, Circle announced
plans to expand into 40 new markets in Southeast Asia, LatAm and the Middle East. 99%

of stablecoins (by market cap) remain pegged to USD and are largely backed by US
Treasuries.
Looking ahead, the key debate to watch is whether US regulations will allow for yield-
bearing stablecoins. Research shows that consumers say policymakers should prohibit
crypto companies from offering interest-like rewards for holding stablecoins, which will
threaten to siphon off the bank deposits that drive local lending. The US CLARITY Act
failed in Sep after lawmakers failed to agree on whether the broader crypto market

structure would support such yield-bearing stablecoins.
In this chartbook, we revisit the stablecoin market a year after the GENIUS Act was
signed. We look at: (i) how adoption has evolved, (ii) the emergence of stablecoins as
financial market plumbing via liquidity provisioning and collateral management, and (iii)
the key regulatory debates that will determine the industry’s next direction.
Payment volume has a +66% CAGR

### Page 3

Marion Laboure | Camilla Siazon |  STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?  |  October  20, 2026
REFRESHER: What are stablecoins?
– Definition: Digital tokens pegged to “stable” assets (e.g. USD or gold), issued
and transferred on a blockchain, which records ownership and transactions.
– Fiat-backed stablecoins are considered fully “collateralized”: USD
stablecoin with 1mn coins in circulation would be backed by $1mn in
cash and equivalents held in reserve by the stablecoin issuer.

– Types: Fiat-, commodity-, crypto- and algorithmic-backed.
– ~99% of stablecoins market valuation is pegged to the USD.
– Market cap: $5bn (2020) to ~$310bn (2026)
– Concentrated in USDT (~60%) and USDC (~23%).
– Benefits: 24/7 transfers & settlement, lower financial intermediaries,
programmability (e.g. escrow, conditional release, automated reconciliation),
and dollar-based store of value.
– Use cases:*
– Crypto trading (~77%) remains dominant.

– Payments (5-12%) growing, cross-border transfers and remittances.
– Store of value (17-33%) especially in emerging markets.
– Other: (~2%-8%): on/off ramps and tokenized RWA settlement.
– Alternatives: CBDCs, tokenized deposits, traditional financial architecture
*Estimates on market share taken from BCG analysis.
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Haver Analytics, Bloomberg Finance LP. Shows stablecoin total
market cap. Updated October 19 2026.
Stablecoins’ reached a plateau of ~$310bn in October 2026

### Page 4

Marion Laboure | Camilla Siazon |  STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?  |  October  20, 2026
ADOPTION OVERVIEW: Most stablecoin volume is still not driven by real economic
activity
Stablecoin transaction volume:
– ~$100trn (unadjusted), ~$2.42trn (adjusted) in 2026 YTD, according to
Allium.
– Chainalysis places more bullish estimates of $28trn in 2025; projected to
reach $719trn by 2035 on organic growth alone.
Stablecoin payments:
– Stablecoin payment volume grew from $151bn in 2022 to $375bn 2026 YTD

(according to VisaOnChain Analytics).
– Businesses account for the majority (58%-64%) of payments, led by B2B
payment volumes.
– Allium Sep report showed largest business payment categories include
service fees ($56bn), payroll ($43bn) and service payments ($32bn).
– Case study: Payments volume across Visa’s >160 stablecoin-linked card
programs across consumer, business and commercial activity grew ~200%
y/y.
Strongest adoption in remittances, B2B payments and dollar access in countries

with unstable currencies or weak banking systems.
– Benefits for cross-border use cases: Instant settlement, <$0.10 per
transaction, borderless, transparent, 24/7, no intermediaries. Remittance fees
are lower (0.5-3.0%) than the global fee average (~6.6%).
– Stablecoins represented 0.31% of fiat cross-border payment value in 2025.
Marginal portion but registered +64% growth increase vs fiat (+9%) according
to Allium. B2B payments had the highest cross-border share (43%).

– A 2025 IMF study also shows transaction flows reached  ~7.7% of GDP in
Latin America and ~6.7% in Africa and the Middle East.
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Allium. Shows top 8 countries by received payments from Jan to August 2026.
Only the receiving end is attributed, so a $100 payment counts once, not twice. That attribution covers 13.5% of
stablecoin payments.
